**(m‴) ADDENDUM — THE TUBE (2026-11-01 08:5x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; the parting experiment iii-73 named, run in its cheapest form within the hour; cup_box.c CUP_ENDS, the record cup_tube_L3.2026-11-01a.txt, its stake file written before the number).** The box 3 × 1 × 1 with its two END faces as the walls (spin 1) and its twelve long-side faces as the cup — an ANNULUS, χ = 0, attached along TWO cycles, the walls' eight boundary links its legs at ½ — reads 1.525878906 × 10⁻⁵ at the uniform assignment and 3.725290298 × 10⁻⁹ at j = 3/2: THE RATIO 2⁻¹² TO TEN DIGITS, the cup's d^{χ − n} with χ = 0 and n = 12, where "one power per wall link" said 2⁻⁸ and "plaquettes plus boundary links less one" said 2⁻⁹ — the three laws parted three ways on one run, and the Euler characteristic entered as the law says (a disc of twelve faces reads 2⁻¹¹). So the neck's mixed side weighs by its OWN Euler count whatever its attachment cycles: the factor (d_½/d_j)^{n − χ} on the side against the uniform assignment, which is the gas's cut identity S4′ read on the mixed side as a cut piece — a check, W = 0, not a discovery. The general attachment-cycle lemma the neck owed is therefore S4′'s own statement on an orientable side, the tube's two cycles included, and what remains open there is what remains open for S4′ itself: the non-orientable piece (A1). The L × M × 1 box and the doubled-wall control are not needed for this question; the exact cup and tube amplitudes stand as (m)'s instances, their coefficient factors as (m″) wrote them.


**(n) A1 MADE EXACT — S4′ HOLDS WITH ITS OWN C(T) ON EVERY CLOSED BRANCH-FREE SURFACE, ORIENTABLE OR NOT (2026-11-01 10:4x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; a desk lemma with its check, written while the campaign's runs return).** §15's A1 proved S4′ on a non-orientable rest as an INEQUALITY, C(T) ≤ the orientable value, because the signs of N₂'s terms were left open; (m‴) ADDENDUM named "what remains open for S4′ itself: the non-orientable piece" as the neck lemma's last open piece. It closes here as an identity, by the sign of the adjoint representation.

THE FOUR GLUING RULES of a Haar link integral on SU(2) (D = D^j, d = 2j + 1, X and Y fixed group elements): (G1) ∫da χ(Xa)χ(a⁻¹Y) = χ(XY)/d — two faces merged along a link traversed oppositely; (G1′) ∫da χ(Xa)χ(Ya) = χ(XY⁻¹)/d — two faces merged along a link traversed in the SAME direction; (G2) ∫da χ(aXa⁻¹Y) = χ(X)χ(Y)/d — a link within one face, traversed oppositely; (G2′) ∫da χ(aXaY) = (−1)^{2j} χ(XY⁻¹)/d — a link within one face, traversed in the same direction, a cross-cap. Proof of the primed rules: the fundamental's pseudo-reality, conj D(a) = ε D(a) ε⁻¹ with ε_j = D^j(ε) real and ε_jᵀ = (−1)^{2j} ε_j, and Schur's ∫ D_{mn} conj D_{rs} = δ_{mr}δ_{ns}/d, give ∫da D(a)_{mn} D(a)_{pq} = ε_{pm}(ε⁻¹)_{nq}/d. Contracting with X_{nm}Y_{qp} (two faces) gives tr(X·(ε⁻¹Yε)ᵀ)/d = tr(X·conj(Y)ᵀ)/d = tr(XY†)/d = χ(XY⁻¹)/d, no sign; contracting with X_{np}Y_{qm} (one face) gives tr(X ε Yᵀ ε⁻ᵀ)/d = (−1)^{2j} tr(X εYᵀε⁻¹)/d = (−1)^{2j} χ(XY⁻¹)/d, since ε⁻ᵀ = (−1)^{2j}ε⁻¹ and εYᵀε⁻¹ = Y⁻¹ for unitary Y. (G1) and (G2) are Schur's lemma as S4′ used it.

THE SURFACE LAW. For a closed connected surface T of n faces carrying bare characters χ_j, integrating its links one at a time by the rules — (G1), (G1′) merge two faces (n − 1, χ unchanged, a factor 1/d), (G2) splits a face (n + 1, χ + 2, 1/d), (G2′) keeps the face count and raises χ by one ((−1)^{2j}/d) — ends at faces with empty words, χ_j(1) = d each, and gives Haar(T) = (−1)^{2j·m} d^{χ(T) − n}, m the number of (G2′) steps; m mod 2 does not depend on the order (the integral does not), and m is the number of cross-caps of T: RP² one (the Frobenius–Schur indicator ∫χ_j(a²) = (−1)^{2j}), the Klein bottle two (the one-face word a a b b reads 1/d). For an orientable T, m = 0: iii-73's d^χ per sheet.

THE CUT IDENTITY, EXACT. In S4′'s proof: (1) a non-orientable piece P integrates to (−1)^{2j·m(P)} d^{χ(P)−n(P)} Π_b χ_½(z_b), z_b the holonomy of the boundary walk (a class function, orientation-free); the sign enters K and K does not enter C = N₂/N₁². (2) N₁ = ∫_T Π_b χ_½(z_b) is the Haar weight, at spin ½, of T's ribbon surface capped by one disc per curve — a closed surface with χ(T) + #curves and #curves faces — so N₁ = ±2^{χ(T)} (the sign that §15 found), N₁² = 4^{χ(T)}. (3)–(4) The corner argument is orientation-free: around a vertex cycle the T-links and the corners alternate whatever the local orientations, so an even A is a union of whole components' curve sets. (5) Each term of N₂ is the Haar weight AT SPIN 1 of K's capped ribbon, (−1)^{2·1·m(K)} 3^{χ(K)} = +3^{χ(K)}: the adjoint is REAL — its Frobenius–Schur indicator is +1 — so every term is positive on every ribbon, orientable or not. Hence N₂ = Π_K (1 + 3^{χ(K)}) and C(T) = Π_K 4^{−χ(K)}(1 + 3^{χ(K)}) = Π_K [4^{b₁(K)−1} + (4/3)^{b₁(K)−1}] exactly, on every closed branch-free surface S. ∎ §15's "≤" is "="; S10′ and the cycle-gluing cost 2 used only the bound and are unchanged; the neck's general attachment-cycle lemma has no non-orientable remainder — the mixed side's factor is (−1)^{2j·m} d^{χ−n} with the sign squaring away wherever the L² identity is used and standing wherever the weight itself is read (the one-sided wall of S7 its one-wall instance).

THE CHECK (crosscap.c, the engine's own quadrature rule and representation matrices copied so the file composes nothing; the record crosscap.2026-11-01a.txt): the four rules at j = ½, 1, 3/2, 2 for fixed X and Y of no special form, residuals at most 6 × 10⁻¹⁶ at rule degrees 12 and 16 (the integrand has degree 4j, the rule is exact to its degree, so a residual at roundoff is a proof of the rule's value and a residual of order one would be a wrong law); the Frobenius–Schur indicator (−1)^{2j} and the Klein bottle 1/d at each j. W = 0: the four rules are the gluing rules of two-dimensional Yang–Mills theory (Migdal 1975; Witten 1991, the non-orientable surfaces with the Frobenius–Schur indicator), and the earning is S4′ exact where §15 had the bound, by one observation — the sign lives in the half-integer spins and N₂ is computed in the adjoint.

**(n) SIGNED (iii-73, 10:5x EST, by message; entered by the trunk seat).** "(n) is the Haar law as I know it": a same-direction link is glued through the fundamental's self-duality D^j ≅ (D^j)* by the ε-tensor, whose symmetry is the Frobenius–Schur indicator ν_j = (−1)^{2j}, so a closed connected sheet with m cross-caps weighs ν_j^m d^χ per bare-character face count — Witten 1991's non-orientable term Σ_R ν_R^m (dim R)^{χ(Σ)}, Migdal 1975 for the orientable case. His two sharpenings, both right and entered: (i) THE SIGN TEST IS RP² AT HALF-INTEGER SPIN ALONE — the Klein bottle and the torus cannot see it (ν² = 1 = ν⁰), so a rule check passing on orientable sheets and at m = 2 has not tested the sign; the check's Frobenius–Schur line IS the m = 1 case at j = ½ and 3/2 (∫χ_j(a²) = −1 read at both) and the (G2′) rule itself at j = ½ and 3/2 with generic X, Y is a second sign test (its value −0.0368 at j = ½ against +0.0368 for the unsigned law, the residual 4 × 10⁻¹⁷), and the record names them as such from here on. (ii) THE CUT IDENTITY'S REASON stated as the reason: N₂ is computed in the adjoint, which is real (ν₁ = +1) for every cross-cap count, so each term is +3^{χ(K)} and no orientability enters; the identity is forced on every closed branch-free surface; the one place a sign could survive is a half-integer spin in a cut piece, and (n)'s cut uses none.


**(o) THE TAIL FACTOR, RE-MEASURED ON A SECOND ROAD, AND WHAT A PROOF OF IT WOULD TAKE (2026-11-01 11:1x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; a desk reading with its instrument, made because a side reader named "the tail factor that moves the proved β*" as the lever to weigh the campaign's machine time against).** The fold-in's MU = 5 is the closing tree's branching bound (kp_reach6.py: "the tail N(F) ≤ 5^(F−1) (closing tree; PROVED)"), and §14's table reads "a proved tail of 4.33 would give 0.54". THE INSTRUMENT (`surf2.c`, surf.c's growth verbatim — the smallest open link closed by one of the five other plaquettes at it, a plaquette refused if in P or if a link would carry three — with the size pruning removed and the NODES counted per depth: the admissible-candidate histogram, the grandchildren maximum, the refusals by kind; the closed leaves met as surf.c's known answer, 4, 60, 80 at F = 6, 10, 12 MET; record `surf2_d4_D13.2026-11-01a.txt`). THE READING: the level ratio n_k/n_{k−1} runs 5, 5, 4.92, 4.837, 4.834, 4.804, 4.808, 4.806, 4.806, 4.805, 4.805 to depth 11 and 4.8053 at depths 12 and 13 (170,259,193 and 818,144,349 nodes; the F = 14 leaves, 1,092, MET at depth 13; record a's admitted-fold count at depth 13 overflowed MinGW's 32-bit `long` and reads negative there — the branching counts are below 2³¹ and unaffected — and record b re-counts with 64-bit counters) — iii-38's 16.50 read 4.7628 at depth 13 for the ALIVE nodes of the same tree (the dead ends and the unclosable excluded), the same shape by depth (5, 5, 4.92, 4.81, 4.79, 4.77, …), so the two roads agree on the tree and part by their populations, as the definitions say they should; the minimising rule's tree (16.50 (v): the open link minimising Σ_q x^{2−2j(q)} at x = 2^{−1/6}) is the one that branches at 4.34, the "4.33" of §14's table. TWO FACTS THE HISTOGRAMS ADD: (1) at depth 11, 82.4 % of the nodes have all five candidates admissible, 15.8 % four, 1.7 % three, under 0.5 % two or fewer; the refusals are 93.8 % folds (a perpendicular candidate whose far links meet a doubly-covered link of a nearby plaquette) and 6.2 % coplanar; (2) THE GRANDCHILDREN MAXIMUM IS 25 AT EVERY DEPTH READ (3.07 × 10⁶ of the 7.37 × 10⁶ nodes at depth 10 have it), and a straight strip of any length has five admissible candidates at every one of its boundary links — so for the smallest-link rule no per-node and no two-step bound below 5 exists, and for the minimising rule the one local lemma that would give 4.762 (16.50 (v)) is false on the reached nodes (16.51): the rate below 5 is an entropy statement (most paths meet refusals), not a local one. WHAT A PROOF WOULD TAKE, therefore: a pattern theorem of Kesten's kind (a local pattern that forces a refusal occurs a positive fraction of the time on all but exponentially few paths), which gives μ < 5 STRICTLY with an explicit but poor constant, nothing like 4.3; whether a k-step bound exists for some larger k is exactly what such a theorem decides, and nothing to depth 13 suggests a small k. THE RIGOROUS LEVERS THAT EXIST: (a) the anchored tail, N(F) ≤ A(13)·5^{F−14} with A(13) the exact alive count — already in the number (16.50 (iii), the factor 0.6165); (b) THE EXACT COUNTS TO A LARGER F₀ — each plaquette of F₀ multiplies the tail by 5u ≈ 0.68 (16.50 (iii)), and J8's number moved 0.4709 → 0.4759 from F₀ = 20 to 22; F₀ = 24 asks the whole census chain (the surfaces, the contact leaves, the orientability, the weights by profile: g48_polymers_v7, g49_oddtree, g50_weights, g51_orient) at area 24, about 24 times area 22's nodes, a day on this host's 32 cores if the instruments split on the depth-two subtrees (each object is reached once, so the counts add without a shared table) — PRICED HERE, not started: the campaign's runs own the cores through the afternoon, and the gain is of order 0.005–0.01 in β*. So the side reader's lever is real, compute-bound, and small; the tail's RATE is a theorem no afternoon makes. W = 0 (the tree is surf.c's; the structural facts are measurements).


**(p) THE RESOLUTION FORMULA — EVERY ALL-½ SET'S WEIGHT AS A SIGNED SUM OVER THE RESOLUTIONS OF ITS CONTACT LINKS, AND S12's REMAINDER AS THE FINITE INEQUALITY IT IS (2026-11-01 12:1x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; a desk lemma on (n) with its instrument, checked on the census's own leaves).** THE LEMMA. Let X be a closed set of plaquettes in ℤ⁴ with every link of multiplicity 2 or 4 (N₄ of the latter), each plaquette carrying χ_½. At a link traversed by four plaquettes the Haar integral of four spin-½ matrix elements is Creutz's ∫dU U_{i₁j₁}U_{i₂j₂}U_{i₃j₃}U_{i₄j₄} = (1/6)[ε_{i₁i₂}ε_{i₃i₄}ε_{j₁j₂}ε_{j₃j₄} + (13|24) + (14|23)] (Creutz 1978; its trace is 2 = dim Inv(½^{⊗4}), checked), while a two-plaquette link integrates to (1/2)εε; so the four-plaquette integral is (2/3) TIMES THE SUM OVER THE THREE PAIRINGS of the two-plaquette integrals of the pairs, for any orientations of the passages (an inverse passage is ε-conjugated as in (n), which moves ε's onto the external indices and leaves the pairing structure). Applying it at every four-link: ∫Π_{p∈X}χ_½(U_p) = (2/3)^{N₄} Σ_{ρ} Π_{T∈ρ} (−1)^{m(T)} 2^{χ(T)−n(T)}, the sum over the 3^{N₄} RESOLUTIONS ρ (one pairing at each four-link), each a disjoint union of closed connected branch-free surfaces T priced by (n)'s law (χ its Euler characteristic as an abstract surface — its vertices the umbrellas of plaquettes around each lattice site glued along the paired passages — m its cross-cap count, n its plaquettes). In the page's units (a sphere reads 4): S(X) := W/u^{|X|} = (2/3)^{N₄} Σ_ρ Π_T (−1)^{m(T)} 2^{χ(T)}. ∎ W = 0 (Creutz's integral; the resolution picture is the loop-model form of the spin-½ Haar weight); the earning is the closed form of every term through (n), the sign included, which makes the weight of ANY such set a finite signed enumeration with no integral left.

THE MINIMALITY LEMMA. X is Z₂-minimal (no proper nonempty closed subset) iff every resolution of X is connected. Proof: a proper closed Y ⊂ X has, at each four-link, its plaquettes among the four in an even number (0, 2 or 4), so pairing Y's two with each other where there are two gives a resolution in which Y and X∖Y are never glued — disconnected; conversely a disconnected resolution's component is a closed subset of X with even multiplicities, proper, so X is not minimal. ∎ (On the census: the pinch's three resolutions all connected; the two-box waist's 78 of 81, the three disconnected ones its two spheres — it is a composite, as §14 read it.)

THE RECURSION. Resolving one four-link ℓ of X in its three ways gives three abstract complexes X₁, X₂, X₃ (two copies of ℓ, each of multiplicity 2) with S(X) = (2/3)[S(X₁) + S(X₂) + S(X₃)]; there is no linear relation among the three (the binor identity relates ε-pairs, the pairing tensors are products of two), so the recursion is all the structure the link gives.

THE CHECK (resolve.c, the trunk's reader of the g48 records' plaquette lists; records/surface-gas/resolve/): a cube 4 (one resolution, a sphere); two cubes sharing an edge 16 = 4 · 4 (the three resolutions: two spheres χ = 4, and twice one sphere — the "touching: 1" law of §13); THE F = 18 PINCH OF THE CENSUS reads 4 as g50 read all 576 — its three resolutions are TWO SPHERES AND ONE PROJECTIVE PLANE, 4 + 4 − 2 = 6, and the sign is load-bearing: unsigned, 10 · (2/3) = 6.67; every pinch listed at F = 18 and F = 20 (10) reads 4 and every two-box waist (16) reads 32 = (2/3)⁴ · 162 with 78 of 81 resolutions connected and 36 spheres among them, g50's 32/1. (A sphere with a contact whose cross-resolution splits an umbrella at one end and merges at the other is a second sphere; the third resolution is then forced to χ = 1 by the two ±1 changes of the vertex count, hence RP², hence −2: the pinch's 4 + 4 − 2 is a theorem of the vertex arithmetic, not a coincidence.)

WHAT IT MAKES OF S12's REMAINDER. §18 proved |W_½(X)| ≤ u^{|X|}2^{χ(Σ)} when every component of the contact graph on the sheet has cycle rank at most one, and STAKED it beyond. By the lemma the remainder is the finite statement: for every Z₂-minimal X, |Σ_ρ Π_T (−1)^{m(T)} 2^{χ(T)}| ≤ (3/2)^{N₄} · 2^{χ(Σ)} — a signed sum over 3^{N₄} connected closed surfaces, each term a power of two with (n)'s sign, against the sheet's power. Two things the formula settles at once: (i) every resolution of a minimal set is connected, so every term is at most 4 in absolute value and the weak form |S(X)| ≤ 4 · 2^{N₄} is immediate (S11's kind of bound, no better); (ii) the proof cannot be termwise — the pinch needs its RP²'s minus sign — so what is to be proved is a CANCELLATION statement about the resolutions of one link against each other, which the recursion localises: S(X) = (2/3)Σ_i S(X_i) with the X_i's resolutions those of X. THE INSTRUMENT THIS NAMES: the odd-rule tree (g49's) restricted to a box, every Z₂-minimal leaf with a contact component of cycle rank two or more (none to F = 20 on the whole lattice; a box reaches larger areas) read by resolve.c — the first readings of the staked case, each exact; a reading above 2^{χ(Σ)} refutes the strong stake, above 4 the weak one and with it Theorem 8.9's tail as priced.


**(p) STAKE — THE BOX SEARCH, WRITTEN BEFORE ITS FIRST LINE IS READ (2026-11-01 12:3x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; boxenum.c launched 12:29 on the boxes 2×4×2×2 to F = 22 and 3×3×2×1 to F = 24, a search through the corner plaquette, not a census).** The instrument: the odd-rule tree inside a box (the smallest odd link closed by any plaquette at it that keeps every link at multiplicity ≤ 4; Lemma P's tree, the duplicates hashed as in surf.c), every leaf read by the resolution formula, minimality decided by the resolutions' connectivity, the two forms of S12's remainder checked on every minimal leaf: STRONG |S(X)| ≤ 2^{χ_max} (χ_max over the resolutions: the sheet's), WEAK |S(X)| ≤ 4. Its sanity on three small boxes: the unit tesseract's two cube boundaries through p₀ (F = 6, S = 4), the 2×1×1×1 and 2×2×1×1 boxes to F = 16 with every minimal leaf a surface reading 2^χ and every contact leaf (N₄ ≥ 1, F ≤ 16) reducible — as the lattice-wide census (pinches from F = 18) says. THE STAKE, three readings deep: (S-a) THE CENSUS LAW HOLDS BEYOND THE CENSUS — every minimal leaf found reads S(X) = 2^{χ_max} EXACTLY (4 at a sphere sheet), as the 5,376 leaves to F = 20 did: the two cross-resolutions at every contact sum to half the sheet's weight, as at the pinch (4 − 2 = 2) and at the edge-sharing composite (4 + 4 = 8 = 16/2); the falsifier a minimal leaf with S ≠ 2^{χ_max}. (S-b) If (S-a) fails, the WEAK form still holds: no minimal leaf above 4. (S-c) UNGUESSED: whether a minimal leaf with a contact component of cycle rank two appears in these boxes at all (none to F = 20 lattice-wide; the box is small and the search is through one corner plaquette); if none appears, the search says nothing about the staked case and the boxes grow. What (S-a) would mean if it held on a leaf of the staked kind: S12's remainder is not a bound but an identity, and the proof to seek is of the identity — the half-weight relation at a contact link, which the pairing tensors alone do not give (no linear relation among them) and which must therefore come from the minimality of X. RESULTS follow the runs.


**(p) RESULTS — THE FIRST READING OF THE BOX SEARCH (2026-11-01 12:3x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; records boxenum_2x4x2x2_F22.2026-11-01{a,b}.txt and boxenum_3x3x2x1_F24.2026-11-01{a,b}.txt, record b with the cycle-rank column; 6 s and 20 s).** (S-a) HELD ON EVERY MINIMAL LEAF READ: 41,004 minimal closed sets through the corner plaquette in the first box to F = 22 and 90,684 in the second to F = 24 — among them the contact leaves 80 / 664 / 4,202 at F = 18 / 20 / 22 in the first and 64 / 512 / 2,983 / 13,681 at F = 18 / 20 / 22 / 24 in the second, with one, two and (from F = 20) three contact links (400 with three at F = 24) — and EVERY ONE reads S(X) = 4 = 2^{χ_max} to the digit; the maximum |S| over the minimal leaves is 4 at every area and every contact count. (S-b) HELD a fortiori: no minimal leaf above 4. (S-c) THE STAKED KIND DID NOT APPEAR, and the census says why: the contact graph on the sheet of EVERY minimal contact leaf read is a forest (the largest cycle rank of a component is 0 at all 131,688 minimal leaves) — a cycle of contact edges on the sheet is a closed lattice path of length at least four, so cycle rank one needs at least four preimage edges in one component (two contact links at the least) placed on a closed path, and cycle rank two at least seven (four contact links); and every leaf with four or more contact links to F = 24 in these boxes is REDUCIBLE (346, 28, 43 at F = 22 with 4, 5, 6 four-links, 1,366, 454, 61, 26 at F = 24 with 4 to 7 — none minimal). So the search's first reading says, exactly: the census law S(X) = 2^{χ(Σ)} holds on every Z₂-minimal all-½ set reached, 131,688 of them to area 24, where the lattice-wide census had 5,376 to area 20; the staked case needs a minimal set with at least four contact links forming cycles on its sheet, which these boxes do not hold to these areas; the boxes 3×3×2×1 to F = 26 and 28, 3×3×2×2 and 4×3×2×1 to F = 26 are in flight (the enumerator prints every leaf of the staked kind with its plaquettes; the cost of F = 28 about three hours). The reading is an exact census of a box through one plaquette, not of the lattice; every leaf is its own exact number.


**(p) RESULTS 2 — THE MECHANISM LOCATED: THE AGGREGATE IDENTITY AT EVERY CONTACT LINK (2026-11-01 12:4x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; boxenum.c with the surgery census, records boxenum_*_F22.2026-11-01{c,d,e}.txt and boxenum_*_F24.2026-11-01{c,d,e}.txt).** For every minimal leaf and every contact link ℓ, with the sheet's pairing at ℓ as the reference, three identities were tested: (A) TERMWISE AT EVERY STAGE — for every assignment of the other contact links, the two cross-resolutions' weights sum to half the sheet-paired one's (W′ + W″ = W_s/2): 55,226 tests, 7,066 failures on 2,533 leaves (13 %), every failure at a MIXED stage where some other link is already crossed (a torus whose cross-surgery at the next link gives a sphere: 4 + 1 against 0.5; a sheet-paired RP² against two spheres: 4 + 4 against −1) — so the termwise identity is not universal, and the census law does not follow termwise. (B) TERMWISE AT THE SHEET STAGE — every other contact link in its sheet pairing: 27,514 tests, 0 failures. (C) THE AGGREGATE IDENTITY PER LINK — the two cross weights SUMMED over all assignments of the other links equal half the sheet-paired sum, Σ_{others} [W′ + W″] = (1/2) Σ_{others} W_s, that is S(X) = S(X_s(ℓ)) with X_s(ℓ) the complex with ℓ resolved in the sheet's way: 27,514 links tested, 0 failures. THE CONSEQUENCE: (C) at one link, iterated over the contact links in any order (each X_s(ℓ) has the same sheet Σ and one contact link fewer; whether it stays Z₂-minimal is immaterial to (C) as tested, which held at every link of every minimal X read — the intermediate complexes' own (C) is what the iteration needs and is UNTESTED beyond the first step), gives S(X) = S(Σ) = 2^{χ(Σ)}: the census law is the aggregate identity's corollary. THE STATEMENT TO PROVE, therefore, in its sharpest tested form: for a Z₂-minimal all-½ set X and a contact link ℓ, Σ over the resolutions ρ′ of the other contact links of [W(ρ′, cross₁) + W(ρ′, cross₂) − W(ρ′, sheet)/2] = 0 — a cancellation that holds only in the sum over ρ′ (13 % of its terms fail), which is the shape of a character-sum orthogonality over the choices at the other links (iii-73's remark of 12:5x: the three pairings at a link as the three ways of choosing the Z₂-twist across it, the signed sum a character sum over the twist group), and the failures at mixed stages the terms that orthogonality cancels. W = 0 on the instrument; the identity (C) is the tree's own finding, measured on 27,514 contact links of 131,688 minimal sets and refuted on none; (A)'s failures are what any proof must account for. The box records c, d, e carry the three counts.


**(p) THE AGGREGATE IDENTITY IN ITS INVARIANT FORM — ONE LINEAR CONDITION ON THE REST OF THE COMPLEX SEEN FROM THE LINK (2026-11-01 12:5x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; a desk computation on (p), no run).** Fix a contact link ℓ of X with endpoints u, v and its four plaquettes 1, 2 (the sheet pair) and 3, 4 (the other sheet pair). Integrating every link but ℓ leaves THE REST R_ℓ: a tensor with the four passages' eight matrix indices open, four at each end, invariant under the gauge group at u and at v separately (every other link at u or v is integrated), hence an element of Inv(½^{⊗4})_u ⊗ Inv(½^{⊗4})_v — a FOUR-dimensional space, since dim Inv(½^{⊗4}) = 2. That space is spanned at each end by the three pairing invariants v_s = ε₁₂ε₃₄, v_{c₁} = ε₁₃ε₂₄, v_{c₂} = ε₁₄ε₂₃, which satisfy the binor relation v_{c₂} = v_{c₁} − v_s (so two of them are a basis) with the Gram matrix ⟨v_s, v_s⟩ = ⟨v_{c₁}, v_{c₁}⟩ = 4, ⟨v_s, v_{c₁}⟩ = 2. The three resolution weights at ℓ are the contractions of R_ℓ with v_π ⊗ v_π (the same pairing at both ends: a two-link Haar integral pairs a passage with another at both ends), and in the coordinates R_ℓ = Σ r_{ij} v_i ⊗ v_j over the basis (v_s, v_{c₁}) at each end they read W_s = 16r₁₁ + 8(r₁₂ + r₂₁) + 4r₂₂, W_{c₁} = 4r₁₁ + 8(r₁₂ + r₂₁) + 16r₂₂, W_{c₂} = 4r₁₁ − 4(r₁₂ + r₂₁) + 4r₂₂. Hence the identity's defect is D(ℓ) := W_{c₁} + W_{c₂} − W_s/2 = 18 r₂₂, and with v_⊥ := 2v_{c₁} − v_s = v_{c₁} + v_{c₂} the invariant orthogonal to v_s (the state in which the sheet pair 1, 2 and the pair 3, 4 each carry total spin ONE, coupled to zero), ⟨R_ℓ, v_⊥ ⊗ v_⊥⟩ = 36 r₂₂ = 2D(ℓ). So: THE AGGREGATE IDENTITY AT ℓ HOLDS IFF THE REST OF THE COMPLEX, SEEN FROM ℓ, HAS NO AMPLITUDE FOR THE SHEET PAIR TO CARRY SPIN ONE ACROSS ℓ AT BOTH ENDS — one linear condition, r₂₂ = 0, on a four-dimensional space, read off the three surface weights as D(ℓ) = 0. (The waist shows the condition is not automatic: S(X) = 32 against the sheet's 16, so D ≠ 0 at some step of its chain.) The three-term sum is therefore not a coincidence of the pairing tensors (no linear relation among the products v_π ⊗ v_π) but a statement about X: the enumerator's 27,514 contact links of minimal sets all have r₂₂ = 0 in the aggregate over the other links' resolutions, 13 % of them not termwise. What a proof must show, in these words: for a Z₂-minimal all-½ set, the amplitude of the spin-1 channel of the sheet pair across any contact link, summed over the resolutions of the other contact links, vanishes — and the termwise reading says the summation is essential, the other links' crossed resolutions carrying spin-1 amplitude that the sheet-paired ones cancel. The sheet-stage half of it (every other link in its sheet pairing: 27,514 of 27,514) is the surgery lemma on ONE closed surface and two of its edges — disjoint edges, edges sharing one vertex, edges on different components all give W′ + W″ = W_s/2 by the vertex count (each cross changes the umbrella count by ±1 at each end) and the sign rule of (n); the digon case (two edges with the same two endpoints) is the one the desk has not closed and the enumerator has not refuted. W = 0 on the invariant theory (the two-dimensional invariant space and the binor relation are textbook); the condition r₂₂ = 0 and its equivalence to the census law are this tree's.


**(p) RESULTS 3 — THE LARGER BOXES: 2.9 MILLION MINIMAL LEAVES TO AREA 28, EVERY ONE EXACTLY 2^χ, AND THE STAKED KIND STILL OUT OF REACH (2026-11-01 12:5x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; records boxenum_3x3x2x1_F26, boxenum_3x3x2x2_F26, boxenum_4x3x2x1_F26 and boxenum_3x3x2x1_F28, letter a, 2 to 7 minutes each).** The three boxes to F = 26 hold 353,539, 735,760 and 358,167 Z₂-minimal all-½ sets through the corner plaquette, the thin box to F = 28 holds 1,289,473 — 2,736,939 minimal leaves in all, with the earlier two boxes 2,868,627 — and the maximum |S| over the minimal leaves is 4 at every area and every contact count, no violation of the strong form or the weak. MINIMAL LEAVES WITH FOUR CONTACT LINKS APPEAR AT F = 26 in the boxes of extent two in the fourth direction (280 in 3×3×2×2 and in 4×3×2×1 alike; in 3×3×2×1 none to F = 28, every leaf with four or more contact links there reducible — 39,318 with four, 16,181 with five, to 24 with ten), all reading 4; the richest area read, F = 26 in 3×3×2×2: 433,790 surfaces, 99,308 with one contact link, 33,366 with two, 120 with three, 280 with four. THE STAKED KIND (a contact component of cycle rank two on the sheet) DID NOT APPEAR: the contact graph is a forest on every one of the 2.87 million sheets. So the census law S(X) = 2^{χ(Σ)} stands refuted on nothing to area 28 and the first forest-free contact structure lies beyond these boxes; a box of extent two in every direction (3×3×3×3, 2,000 plaquettes) at F = 28 is the next size, its tree about a hundred times this one's. The reading is of the aggregate identity's corollary; the identity itself is (p)'s invariant-form condition r₂₂ = 0 at every contact link, and iii-73's reading of the shape to prove (13:0x, carried as he gave it): the one-shot sum over the twist group μ₂^{N₄} — the three resolutions at a link the identity twist and the two crossings — of a product of local weights is a character sum, and the termwise failures at mixed stages are what the one-shot sum cancels, so a termwise or an iterated proof is the wrong shape by construction.


**(p) CORRECTION — THE CENSUS IS LEMMA S6's CLASS; THE IDENTITY ON IT IS S6; WHAT LIES PAST S6 (2026-11-01 13:5x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; a side reader's note of 13:4x, verified and carried; records boxenum_3x3x3x3_F26.2026-11-01b.txt and boxenum_2x4x2x2_F22.2026-11-01f.txt, the enumerator with a second column).** (1) THE TWO FORESTS. Lemma S6's hypothesis is that the links of multiplicity ≥ 4 form a forest IN THE LATTICE; the enumerator's cycle rank b1max was taken ON THE SHEET (the preimage edges over the contact links, the sheet's corner classes as vertices). The two graphs differ: the sheet graph maps onto the lattice graph two edges to one. The lattice reading is forced for all but 280 of the 2.87 million leaves: ℤ⁴ is bipartite, so a cycle needs four links, and every leaf with at most three contact links is S6's. A second column (b1lat, the cycle rank of the contact links in ℤ⁴) was added and the hypercubic box re-run (record b, 316 s; the F = 22 control against record e equal line for line): b1lat = 0 on all 676,738 minimal leaves, the 280 four-contact leaves at F = 26 included. SO: EVERY MINIMAL SET THE BOXES FOUND IS IN S6's CLASS, the census law S(X) = 2^{χ(Σ)} on it is Lemma S6 (W = 0 here; the side reader's point, verified), and THE AGGREGATE IDENTITY of RESULTS 2–3 is S6 applied twice — to X and to X_s(ℓ), both forest-contact sets with the same sheet — not a finding of this tree; its invariant form (r₂₂ = 0) is the same statement in other words. What the box census is, honestly: a check of S6 on 2.87 million sets and a reading of HOW the 3^{N₄}-term resolution sum realises S6's one-line gauge-fixing (termwise the surgery identity fails at 12–13 % of the mixed stages, the aggregate holds; a reading, no theorem). RESULTS 2's "the identity (C) is the tree's own finding" and RESULTS 3's "the staked kind" paragraph are corrected by this entry; nothing above is edited. (2) THE GAP IN S12 AS WRITTEN. S12's route says a contact cycle "whose two preimages together separate the sheet" makes X a composite. The preimage of a lattice cycle on the sheet need not be a Z₂-cycle at all: at a lattice vertex u the preimage edges end in corner classes, and an umbrella at u may pass through one contact link of the cycle an odd number of times (nothing but the total, four per link, is even). The sentence is replaced by the piece criterion of (4). (3) THE CUT FORMULA IN THE WORDS OF THE CONTACT GRAPH. Let G be the graph of the contact links (multiplicity ≥ 4) in ℤ⁴, X_cut the surface X cut along all of them (its pieces are the components of the plaquette graph on the 2-links), b its boundary circles. Each circle is a closed walk in G; gauge-fix a spanning forest of G to 1, so the r = (cycle rank of G) chord links carry the variables, and the circle's holonomy z_b is the reduced word of its walk in π₁(G) = F_r evaluated at the chords. Then S(X) = 2^{χ(X_cut)} · ∫_{SU(2)^r} Π_b χ_½(z_b) (the surface-with-boundary integral, (n) A1's sign on a non-orientable piece), whence |S| ≤ 2^{χ(X_cut) + n_b} = 2^{χ(X̂)} (S11), and S6 is the case r = 0 (every word trivial). The waist (four discs on one square: four words U, ∫χ_½⁴ = 2, S = 2⁴·2 = 32) is this formula's first check against the record. (4) MINIMALITY AS A RANK. A closed proper subset of X is a union of pieces of X_cut, and a union is closed iff its count of free sides at every contact link is even; writing each piece's odd-set (the links where it has an odd count) as a vector over Z₂, X is Z₂-minimal iff the pieces' odd-sets have rank m − 1 (m the number of pieces; m = 1 is minimal outright). A piece's odd-set is the Z₂-class in G of its boundary walks, hence a Z₂-cycle of G; so a piece whose boundary walks are Z₂-trivial in G is closed alone, and in a minimal X with m ≥ 2 every piece carries a nonzero cycle class. (5) THEOREM S12-a (THE SPHERE WITH A FOREST PREIMAGE). Let X be Z₂-minimal with sheet Σ a sphere on which the preimage P of the contact links is a forest. Then S(X) = 4 = 2^{χ(Σ)}, WHATEVER THE LATTICE CONTACT GRAPH — lattice cycles allowed. Proof: X_cut = Σ − P is a sphere with one hole per tree of P (χ = 2 − n_b), and each boundary circle is the Euler tour of its tree, which traverses every edge once each way in nested order, so its word reduces to 1 for ANY values of the link holonomies — no gauge-fixing needed; every χ_½(z_b) = 2, and S = 2^{2 − n_b} · 2^{n_b} = 4. ∎ S6 ⊂ S12-a on sphere sheets (a lattice forest lifts to a forest on any sheet); the census (b1max = 0 everywhere) is S12-a's class as well, so the aggregate identity on it is also S12-a's corollary (X_s(ℓ) keeps the sphere and a sub-forest). The argument is this tree's; the integral it uses is textbook. (6) ONE CYCLE ON THE SHEET (THE FIRST OBJECT PAST THE CENSUS). If P has exactly one cycle γ on the sphere sheet, X_cut has two pieces (inside, outside γ) with words w and w⁻¹ (γ's class in π₁(G), the trees' tours trivial), and by (4) γ's Z₂-class in G is nonzero, so some chord is traversed by γ exactly once (a simple cycle on Σ uses each of a 4-link's two preimage edges at most once, and an odd count ≤ 2 is 1); that chord appears once in w, and integrating it first gives ∫|χ_½(w)|² = 1 exactly (∫ dU χ(XUY)χ(Y⁻¹U⁻¹X⁻¹) = tr(1)/2 = 1). Hence S = 2^{χ(P₁) + χ(P₂)} ≤ 4, with equality iff both pieces are discs — S12's bound on this class, and the census law's equality with it. The same computation on a theta graph (two cycles, three faces, words a, b, (ab)⁻¹ at the first non-trivial pattern) reads ∫χ(a)χ(b)χ(b⁻¹a⁻¹) = ½ and S = 2³ · ½ = 4 again: a desk reading, not a lemma. THE CONJECTURE these readings suggest and nothing proves: S(X) = 4 for every Z₂-minimal X with a sphere sheet, by the pulled-back integral over Hom(π₁(G), SU(2)) equalling the free one over Hom(π₁(P), SU(2)) (the 2D sphere amplitude 2^{2 − m} against 2^m faces) whenever the pieces' classes have full rank. (7) THE UNICYCLIC LATTICE BOUND, ANY SHEET. If G is one cycle of 4-links with trees attached: every boundary walk has a uniform traversal parity on the cycle (its winding w_b), so by (4) every piece is odd on the cycle and m ≤ 2; z_b = U^{w_b} with Σ_b |w_b| ≤ 4; and the table ∫χ_½(U^{w}) over the windings (2, 1, −1, 0 at w = 0, ±1, ±2, ±4 single; 1, −1, 2, −2 for the pairs (1,1), (1,3), (2,2), (0,2)) with χ(P) ≤ 2 − n_b(P) gives |S| ≤ 4 in every case, equality at zero windings on planar pieces (m = 1) or at one ±1 winding per disc (m = 2); S may be 0 or negative there, so the census law's EQUALITY is not part of this bound. (8) WHAT THE CENSUS SAYS ABOUT THE OBJECT. No minimal leaf to F = 26 (F = 28 in the thin box) carries a cycle in P. The smallest four-contact closed sets are reducible by construction: the 22-plaquette unit cube joined by an eight-plaquette tube to the ten-plaquette brick f × [−1,1]₄ around the cube's bottom face f has its second passes over ∂f on the brick's equator, a second cycle, and the annulus between the two cycles is closed alone (the census's 354 four-contact leaves at F = 22, none minimal). A minimal one needs the four second passes to be pendant on the sheet — four tongues at the edges of f, each from a different pair of directions, the sheet one piece — and lies past F = 28 by the census; it is the next instrument's target (the enumerator seeded with f, its four cube sides and the four tongues' plaquettes, the box and cap around them), and the one-cycle lemma's prediction for it, S = 4 with both pieces discs, is STAKED here before any such set is read.


**(p) STAKE — OBJECT A, THE FIRST MINIMAL SET BUILT OUTSIDE LEMMA S6, WRITTEN BEFORE ITS WEIGHT IS READ (2026-11-01 14:0x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; the construction by hand, the reading by resolve.c on the plaquette list `resolve/objectA.txt`).** THE OBJECT. The square f = [0,1]² in the (1,2)-plane at the origin, C = ∂f its four edges. (i) The tube C × [0,3]₃ (twelve plaquettes; its first unit the cube's four sides). (ii) The tongues: the tube C × [−1,1]₄ (eight plaquettes; at every edge of C the two tongue plaquettes and f and the cube side make FOUR — C's four links are the contact links, a lattice square, so the set is outside S6). (iii) The upper tube from C + e₄ to C − e₃: (C + e₄) × [−1,0]₃ then (C − e₃) × [0,1]₄ (eight). (iv) The lower tube from C − e₄ to C − 2e₃: (C − e₄) × [−2,0]₃ then (C − 2e₃) × [−1,0]₄ (twelve). (v) The junction Z: the boundary of the box [−1,2]₁ × [−1,2]₂ × [−3,3]₃ (at x₄ = 0) with the tunnel [0,1]₁ × [−1,2]₂ × [−2,−1]₃ bored through it in direction 2 — a torus — minus three unit squares: f + 3e₃ on the top, f − e₃ on the tunnel's ceiling, f − 2e₃ on its floor (97 plaquettes), the three tubes arriving at the three holes. F = 138. In the (3,4)-plane picture every tube is C times a path and the three rims could not meet at a product junction without a fourth contact square, which is why the junction is a surface of genus one. THE STAKES, from the cut formula of the CORRECTION entry, (3): (A1) every link but C's four has multiplicity 2 — resolve.c admits the list; N₄ = 4, b1lat = 1. (A2) Z₂-MINIMAL: the pieces of X_cut are f alone and everything else (the three tubes meet in Z), with one and three free sides at every link of C — both odd, rank 1 = m − 1 — so every resolution is connected. (A3) THE WEIGHT: χ(X_cut) = χ(f) + χ(P₂) = 1 + (0 − 3 + 0) = −2, the four boundary circles (f's rim and the three tubes' rims at C) each wind once around C, I = ∫χ_½⁴ = 2, hence S(A) = 2^{−2} · 2 = 1/2 EXACTLY (the signed sum over the 81 resolutions, in the page's units). (A4) THE SHEET: the pairing (f, side)(tongue, tongue) at every link gives disc + Z + annulus, χ = 1 − 3 + 0 = −2, and the two uniform cross pairings the same; the mixed pairings UNGUESSED but expected no higher; so χ_max = −2 and 2^{χ_max} = 1/4 < 1/2: THE CENSUS LAW S = 2^{χ(Σ)} AND THE STRONG BOUND |S| ≤ 2^{χ_max} FAIL ON OBJECT A, while S12's |S| ≤ 4 and the unicyclic bound hold with room. A reading of S other than 1/2 convicts the cut formula or the construction (a stray contact would be refused by name at (A1)); χ_max above −2 convicts (A4)'s expectation and nothing else. Nothing above β = 0.4764 is touched by this; it is the first object of S12's remainder, built as the side reader asked, and it says the remainder's law is not the census law.


**(p) RESULTS — OBJECT A READ: S = 1/2 EXACTLY, MINIMAL, OUTSIDE S6, AND THE CENSUS LAW FAILS ON IT (2026-11-01 14:0x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; `resolve/objectA.txt` through resolve_b.c — resolve.c with its plaquette and link caps raised to 400 and 1,600, the waist re-read 32 first; record `resolve/objectA.2026-11-01a.txt`).** (A1) HELD: 272 links, 268 of multiplicity two and the four of C at four; no stray contact; N₄ = 4 on a lattice square, b1lat = 1 — the first Z₂-minimal set outside Lemma S6, built by hand. (A2) HELD: all 81 resolutions connected, so Z₂-minimal. (A3) HELD TO THE DIGIT: the signed sum of 2^{χ} over the 81 resolutions is 2.53125 = 81/32 and S = (2/3)⁴ · 81/32 = 1/2, the cut formula's value. (A4) HELD: χ runs from −6 to −2 over the resolutions, no sphere, χ_max = −2, so 2^{χ_max} = 1/4 and S = 2 · 2^{χ_max}: THE CENSUS LAW S = 2^{χ(Σ)} IS FALSE ON A MINIMAL SET, AND SO IS THE STRONG STAKE |S| ≤ 2^{χ_max} (the (p) STAKE's S-a, held on 2.87 million forest-class sets, fails at the first set past the forest class); the WEAK stake |S| ≤ 4 (S12's claim) and the unicyclic bound of the CORRECTION's (7) hold with room. What the object says: on the forest class the weight is the sheet's 2^{χ} because every boundary word is trivial; past it the weight is 2^{χ(X_cut)} times a character integral that the sheet's Euler characteristic does not see — here four rims winding once around one square, ∫χ_½⁴ = 2, against a sheet two handles down. S12's remainder is therefore the bound |S| ≤ 4, not the equality, and the equality conjecture of (6) is confined to sphere sheets (Object A's sheet has genus two; the conjecture is untouched by it and untested). iii-73's remark of 13:5x, carried as the shape of that conjecture's proof: the pulled-back integral over Hom(π₁(G), SU(2)) equals the free one when the restriction to the boundary words pushes Haar measure forward to Haar — a surjectivity of the word map onto SU(2)^{m−1} — of which the pieces' full Z₂-rank is the mod-2 shadow; the one-cycle lemma's "some chord exactly once" is that surjectivity for a primitive word, and the twist-group character sum its Fourier side. THE NEXT OBJECT: the sphere-sheet set with a cycle in its contact preimage (the four second passes pendant tongues, each from its own pair of directions), for which the one-cycle lemma stakes S = 4 with both pieces discs; built by the enumerator seeded with f, its four cube sides and the eight tongue plaquettes (the bricks at the edges of f in the directions (2⁻,4⁺), (1⁺,4⁻), (2⁺,4⁺), (1⁻,4⁻), sharing no link with each other or with the cube beyond C), every other link held at multiplicity two by a flag, so that no new contact can form — launched as this entry lands.


**(p) THEOREM S12-b — THE PRIMITIVE-SYSTEM THEOREM, AND WHAT THE SPHERE-SHEET CONJECTURE REDUCES TO (2026-11-01 14:1x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; iii-73's remark of 13:5x made the statement, the proof and the reduction this seat's).** Setting as in the CORRECTION's (3): X Z₂-minimal, G its lattice contact graph with cycle rank r and chords U₁..U_r after a spanning forest is gauge-fixed to 1, X_cut its cut surface with pieces P₁..P_m and boundary circles b whose words z_b ∈ F_r = π₁(G). THEOREM. Suppose the boundary words can be listed as w₁, …, w_k, w_{k+1}, … where (w₁, …, w_k) is a PRIMITIVE SYSTEM of F_r (part of a free basis) and every remaining word is a product of conjugates of the w_i^{±1} and of words trivial in F_r. Then ∫_{SU(2)^r} Π_b χ_½(z_b) = ∫_{SU(2)^k} Π_b χ_½(z_b(g₁, …, g_k)) with g₁..g_k INDEPENDENT Haar variables — the pulled-back integral is the free one. Proof. Complete (w₁..w_k) to a basis (w₁..w_r) of F_r; the map SU(2)^r → SU(2)^r, (U_j) ↦ (w_i(U)), is a bijection (the inverse basis change is a word map too) and preserves Haar measure, since every Nielsen move (U_j ↦ U_j^{−1}, U_j ↦ U_j U_l, a transposition) does; in the new coordinates g_i := w_i(U) the integrand depends on g₁..g_k alone, and integrating out g_{k+1}..g_r gives the claim. ∎ COROLLARIES. (i) The one-cycle lemma of (6): one cycle γ on a sphere sheet gives two words w, w⁻¹ with w containing a chord exactly once, so w is primitive (substitute that chord) and ∫|χ_½(w)|² = ∫|χ_½(g)|² = 1. (ii) Object A: the four rims' words are U, U⁻¹, U, U⁻¹ (one chord, r = 1, the word primitive), the integral the free ∫χ_½⁴ = 2 — the theorem's reading of its S = 1/2. (iii) For a SPHERE sheet with a connected preimage P of m faces, the face words satisfy one relation (their product is 1 up to conjugation), and if m − 1 of them form a primitive system the integral is the 2D sphere amplitude ∫Π_{i<m} χ_½(g_i) · χ_½((g₁⋯g_{m−1})⁻¹) = 2^{2−m}, so S = 2^m · 2^{2−m} = 4. THE REDUCTION. The sphere-sheet conjecture (S = 4 for every Z₂-minimal X with a sphere sheet) is therefore the combinatorial statement: THE FACE WORDS OF THE CONTACT PREIMAGE OF A MINIMAL SPHERE-SHEET SET, ONE DROPPED, FORM A PRIMITIVE SYSTEM OF π₁(G). Minimality gives the mod-2 shadow of this (the odd-sets independent in H₁(G; Z₂), the CORRECTION's (4)), which is necessary and in general not sufficient — a word with a primitive abelianisation need not be primitive — so the conjecture stands exactly on the gap between the Z₂-rank and primitivity, and a counterexample would be a minimal sphere-sheet set whose face words are independent mod 2 and not primitive. The converse direction (a non-primitive system does not preserve Haar) is the Puder–Parzanchevski theorem for a single word and is quoted, not used; W = 0 on the theorem's two ingredients (Haar invariance under Nielsen moves; the 2D sphere amplitude), the theorem's use and the reduction this tree's. Nothing in this entry changes a number on the page.


**(p) STAKE — OBJECT C, THE FIRST MINIMAL SET WITH A CONNECTED CONTACT GRAPH OF CYCLE RANK TWO, WRITTEN BEFORE ITS WEIGHT IS READ (2026-11-01 14:1x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; the plaquette list `resolve/objectC.txt` by a generator, the reading by resolve_b.c).** THE OBJECT. Two squares f₁ = [0,1]² and f₂ = [1,2] × [0,1] in the (1,2)-plane, sharing the middle link ℓ_m = {1} × [0,1]₂; R = ∂([0,2] × [0,1]) their outer six links; G = R ∪ ℓ_m, seven links, cycle rank two, connected. (i) Tube 1: C₁ × [0,3]₃ upward; tube 2: C₂ × [−3,0]₃ downward — at ℓ_m the two tubes' first plaquettes ℓ_m × [0,1]₃ and ℓ_m × [−1,0]₃ are the third and fourth passages beside f₁ and f₂, so ℓ_m is a contact link, and at every outer link f_i, the tube's side and (ii) make four. (ii) The tongues: the rectangle's tube R × [−1,1]₄ (twelve plaquettes). (iii) Two direction-4 tubes carrying the 3-tubes' far rims out of the hyperplane t = 0: (C₁ + 3e₃) × [0,1]₄ and (C₂ − 3e₃) × [−1,0]₄. (iv) Two junction boxes OUTSIDE the hyperplane: box 1 = ∂([−1,3]₁ × [−1,2]₂ × [−3,3]₃) at t = 1 with the direction-2 tunnel [0,2]₁ × [−1,2]₂ × [0,1]₃ bored through it, minus the 2 × 1 hole R + e₄ on the tunnel's floor (the upper tongue's rim), the unit hole f₁ + 3e₃ + e₄ on its top (tube (iii)'s rim) and the unit square [−1,0]² × {3} on its top for (v); box 2 the mirror at t = −1 with the tunnel at z ∈ [−1,0], the hole R − e₄ on the tunnel's ceiling, f₂ − 3e₃ − e₄ on its bottom, and the same corner square on its top. (v) The connecting tube ∂([−1,0]²) × [−1,1]₄ at z = 3 between the two boxes' corner squares. The tubes stand in t = 0 and the boxes in t = ±1, which is how a direction-1 or direction-2 tunnel in the tubes' own hyperplane, whose walls lie in the tubes' planes, is avoided. F = 290; N₄ = 7; 3⁷ = 2187 resolutions. THE STAKES, from the cut formula: (C1) resolve_b.c admits the list — every link but G's seven at multiplicity two; b1lat = 2. (C2) Z₂-MINIMAL: three pieces — f₁ (one side on each of its four links), f₂ (likewise), and the rest (three sides on each outer link, two on ℓ_m; the two boxes, the connecting tube and the four tubes are one piece), odd-sets C₁, C₂, C₁ + C₂ = R of rank 2 = m − 1; so all 2187 resolutions are connected. (C3) THE WEIGHT: with a, b the chords around f₁ and f₂ (ℓ_m in the spanning tree), the six boundary circles read a (f₁'s rim), a (tube 1's rim), b (f₂'s), b (tube 2's), ab (the upper tongue's rim, around the rectangle), ab (the lower tongue's) — THE FIRST NON-ABELIAN WORD INTEGRAL OF THE SERIES: I = ∫χ_½(a)² χ_½(b)² χ_½(ab)² da db = 4/3 (by ∫db χ₁(b)χ₁(ab) = χ₁(a)/3 and χ_½² = χ₁ + 1, computed at the desk twice); χ(X_cut) = 1 + 1 + (−6) = −4 (each box a torus minus three discs, χ = −3, joined by an annulus; the four tubes annuli); hence S(C) = 2^{−4} · 4/3 = 1/12 EXACTLY. (C4) χ_max = −4 (the uniform pairing: two discs, the junction Z of χ = −6, the tongue annulus), the mixed pairings expected no higher; 2^{χ_max} = 1/16 < 1/12, the census law failing again, the weak bound holding with room. A reading other than 1/12 convicts the cut formula's non-abelian step (the integral of χ_½(ab)² against χ_½(a)²χ_½(b)²) or the construction, which (C1) would name.


**(p) RESULTS — OBJECT C READ: S = 1/12 EXACTLY, THE FIRST NON-ABELIAN WORD INTEGRAL MET ON A LATTICE OBJECT; AND THE SEEDED SEARCH FOR THE SPHERE-SHEET OBJECT (2026-11-01 14:1x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; `resolve/objectC.txt`, `resolve/objectC2.txt` and their records through resolve_b.c; `boxseed_objectB_F40.2026-11-01a.txt` through boxenum_e.exe).** (C1) HELD AFTER ONE CORRECTION OF THE CONSTRUCTION, the defect named: the first list carried EIGHT four-links — the connecting tube's square [−1,0]² at z = 3 shares the vertex (0,0,3) with the top hole's square, so the vertical link from (0,0,3,0) to (0,0,3,1) lay in two plaquettes of each tube, a stray contact. With the square moved to [2,3] × [−1,0] (objectC2): 573 links, 566 at two and exactly G's seven at four, b1lat = 2. (C2) HELD: 2187 resolutions, all connected. (C3) HELD TO THE DIGIT: the signed sum 729/512, S = (2/3)⁷ · 729/512 = 1/12. (C4) HELD: χ from −12 to −4, 2^{χ_max} = 1/16 < 1/12 — the census law fails on a second minimal set, the weak bound holds. What was read: the six rims' words a, a, b, b, ab, ab in the two chords, which are a basis, so Theorem S12-b makes the integral the free one in (a, b), ∫χ_½(a)²χ_½(b)²χ_½(ab)² = 4/3 — a value that is not a power of two because the tongue rims around the rectangle pair the two chords non-abelianly; the cut formula's non-abelian step is thereby exact on a lattice object, and past the forest class the weight is 2^{χ(X_cut)} times a word integral that the sheet's χ undercounts (by 2 on Object A, by 4/3 on Object C). THE SEEDED SEARCH: boxenum_e.exe = boxenum.c with BOX_SEED (plaquette tokens planted in place of the corner plaquette) and BOX_NO4 (every link beyond the seed's four-links held at multiplicity two), the corner-plaquette seed reproducing record f line for line as its control. From the thirteen-plaquette seed (f, its four cube sides, the four tongue pairs in the planes (2⁻,4⁺), (1⁺,4⁻), (2⁺,4⁺), (1⁻,4⁻)) in the 5 × 5 × 5 × 5 box to F = 40: 1.04 × 10⁹ nodes in 8.5 minutes, 5,272 closures at F = 30–40, EVERY ONE REDUCIBLE — no minimal leaf, so no sphere-sheet set with a cycle in its contact preimage from this seed at this size. The one-cycle lemma stands as a theorem about a class whose first member is not in hand; the bricks' other tongue planes (ten choices an edge) are the next seeds, and nothing read says the class is empty. The stake for the seeded search's object (S = 4, two discs) is neither held nor refuted: UNREACHED.


**(p) CORRECTION TO THEOREM S12-b — THE HYPOTHESIS NARROWED TO WHAT THE PROOF COVERS (2026-11-01 14:2x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; a side reader's note of 14:3x, verified).** The theorem's hypothesis admitted every remaining boundary word that is "a product of conjugates of the w_i^{±1}". That is broader than the proof: a product of conjugates BY DIFFERENT CONJUGATORS depends on the variables being integrated out — ∫dg₃ χ_½(g₁ g₃ g₂ g₃⁻¹) = χ_½(g₁)χ_½(g₂)/2, not χ_½(g₁g₂) — so the stated equality fails there. THE CLAUSE NOW READS: every remaining boundary word is, up to ONE overall conjugation by an element of F_r, a word in the basis words w₁^{±1}, …, w_k^{±1} (and in words trivial in F_r). Under this clause the change of variables of the proof makes every integrand a class function of g₁..g_k alone, and the proof is complete as written. Every use on the page is of the narrowed kind: the one-cycle lemma (w, w⁻¹), Object A (U, U⁻¹, U, U⁻¹), Object C (a, a, b, b, ab, ab — a basis and a product in it), and the sphere reduction (iii), whose last face word is the inverse of the product of the others up to a single conjugation. Nothing read changes; the reduction of the sphere-sheet conjecture is restated with the narrowed clause: the face words, one dropped, form a primitive system AND the dropped one is a word in them up to one conjugation — the second condition holds on any sphere automatically (the faces' product relation), so the conjecture's remaining gap is primitivity alone, as before.


**(p) THE SECOND ROAD, AND THE STAKE ON SIX-FOLD CONTACTS — WRITTEN BEFORE THE SIX-LINK CENSUS IS READ (2026-11-01 14:2x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; `cut_reader.c` included by boxenum_cut.c = boxenum_g.exe; the control record boxenum_2x4x2x2_F22.2026-11-01h.txt).** THE SECOND ROAD BUILT: the cut formula of the CORRECTION's (3) as a reader on every leaf — the pieces by the two-links, the corner classes of the cut surface, χ(X_cut), the boundary circles walked side by side, the lattice contact graph's cycle rank, the winding of each circle about a chord when the rank is one and the word integral by exact Clebsch–Gordan multiplication of virtual characters (the rank ≥ 2 refused by name: the words need the Weingarten calculus), the sign by the orientability of each piece, and minimality by the rank of the pieces' odd-sets. THE CONTROL: on the 2 × 4 × 2 × 2 box to F = 22, 50,891 leaves read by both roads — the resolution road glues 3^{N₄} closed surfaces and sums their 2^{χ}, the cut road cuts once and integrates words; they share no line — and S AGREES ON ALL 50,891 (0 parted), the two minimality decisions (every resolution connected; the odd-sets of rank m − 1) AGREE ON ALL 50,891 (0 parted), the census tables equal to record f line for line. Law 6 satisfied for (p). THE STAKE ON SIX-FOLD LINKS. Every enumerator so far held links at multiplicity ≤ 4 by its odd rule (the stray-contact guard), so the census never contained a link in six plaquettes, and S12's claim |S| ≤ 4 was read on a class with no six-fold link. The cut formula gives, for a unicyclic contact graph whose cycle carries a six-fold link, pieces odd on the cycle, m ≤ 2 as in the CORRECTION's (7), and windings up to ±3 at that link: the pair (3, 3) reads ∫χ_½(U³)² = ∫(χ_{3/2} − χ_½)² = 2, so TWO DISCS EACH WINDING THREE TIMES AROUND ONE SQUARE WHOSE FOUR LINKS ARE ALL SIX-FOLD would read S = 2^{1+1} · 2 = 8 — above S12's bound. The six-link unicyclic bound is therefore |S| ≤ 8, with 8 attained only by that configuration (or (1, 3)-type pairs reading −4 at most in absolute value otherwise), and whether a Z₂-minimal set realises it in ℤ⁴ is the question: such a disc has a branch point (a corner class of twelve plaquettes) and its three passes must be joined at the square's corners by umbrella arcs through the (3,4)-plaquettes. THE STAKES: (S6-a) the odd rule with six-fold links admitted (BOX_MAX6) finds leaves with a six-fold link in the boxes to F = 22–24; their count UNGUESSED. (S6-b) among the MINIMAL ones every reading has |S| ≤ 4 — HELD if the triple-winding pair does not fit in the boxes read, and a reading above 4 is printed with its plaquettes as THE SEXTUPLE VIOLATION, which would refute S12 as claimed and move the contact leaves' activity in the fold-in from 4 to the cut formula's value. (S6-c) the two roads cannot be compared on these leaves (the resolution road admits 2- and 4-links only); the cut road reads alone, its control the 50,891 agreements above.


**(p) RESULTS — THE SIX-LINK CENSUS TO F = 24, AND THE TWO ROADS ON 256,457 LEAVES (2026-11-01 14:3x EST, read from `date`; the trunk seat; boxsix_2x4x2x2_F22.2026-11-01a.txt and boxsix_3x3x3x3_F24.2026-11-01a.txt through boxenum_g.exe with BOX_MAX6).** (S6-a) SIX-FOLD LINKS APPEAR AT F = 24 AND NOT BELOW: none in the 2 × 4 × 2 × 2 box to F = 22 (9.6 million nodes); in the hypercubic box to F = 24 (75 million nodes, 54 s) two leaves carry a six-fold link, BOTH REDUCIBLE, so no minimal set with a six-fold link exists through a corner plaquette of that box at that area. (S6-b) HELD VACUOUSLY: no minimal six-link leaf, no reading, no violation; the stake stands open above F = 24 (the triple-winding pair needs at least twelve plaquettes per disc at the square and a branch point in each, so it lies past F = 24 by construction — the star-seeded search is the instrument, launched: all six plaquettes at each edge of one square planted, every other link held at two, the box 5 × 5 × 5 × 5 to F = 40). (S6-c) THE TWO ROADS, EXTENDED: with six-fold links admitted the 2/4-link leaves are the same sets as before (the census tables equal line for line to F = 24), and on them the cut road agrees with the resolution road on all 256,457 compared, in S and in minimality (0 parted each); 40 leaves to F = 24 have contact graphs of cycle rank two or more and are refused by the cut road by name (their words need the Weingarten step, not built) while the resolution road reads them — those 40 are the first sets past the unicyclic class in the census and all were read S ≤ 4 by the resolution road, none minimal with a cycle on the sheet (the b1max table). What this adds: the second road's agreement on a quarter of a million sets makes the cut formula an instrument of record for (p), and the only class the resolution road cannot read (six-fold links) is empty below F = 24.
THE STAR-SEEDED SEARCH, read the same minute (boxsix_star_F40.2026-11-01a.txt, boxenum_h.exe): the whole star of one square planted — f and the five other plaquettes at each of its four edges, twenty-one plaquettes, every link of C six-fold — every other link held at two, the 5 × 5 × 5 × 5 box to F = 40: 1.5 million nodes and NO CLOSED LEAF — the star does not close at all below F = 41 in that box. The triple-winding pair uses the whole star, so it has F > 40 or needs a larger box: (S6-b) is UNREACHED by the instruments of this hour, not held; the question is carried as the first open item of S12's remainder, with the bound |S| ≤ 8 on the unicyclic six-fold class proved by the cut formula's table and the equality's realisability open.
